## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear route: offering terms, mission, platform, then space, connectivity, AI, growth, financials and an appendix, with section-opener photo slides and consistent dark styling, a single typeface and a steady footer. Charts carry the argument directly, with headline claims as titles and big numerals (slides 13, 24, 29, 47 to 54). Weak spots are some text over photos with low contrast (slides 7, 31, 42, 43), a few tiny captions and labels (slides 6, 27, 36, 52 to 54), and a few busy slides (9, 29, 36). The extremely dense disclaimer on slide 2 and the grey-on-black tables are acceptable for a roadshow read, but they are hard to read.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the headline-claim titles, large numerals, the stepped market bar on slide 13, the cost comparison on slide 24 with its percentage callouts, and the three-panel layout on slide 29.

**Most useful repair:** Fix legibility where text sits on photos or is very small, including slides 7, 31, 36, 42 and 43, by adding a dark panel or raising size and contrast.

**What would change the score:** I would thin the smaller gray captions and the run of near-identical reconciliation tables, and add a clear takeaway line to the financial slides 47 to 54.

_Confidence high. 60 of 60 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck is a roadshow presentation for SpaceX's proposed initial public offering, dated June 2026. It opens with a forward-looking statements disclaimer, then an offering summary covering issuer, size, price, use of proceeds and lock-up terms, followed by the company mission and an integrated platform overview across space, connectivity and AI. The main body runs through the space business (launch vehicles, reusability, cost per kilogram), the connectivity business (Starlink broadband and mobile), and the AI business (compute infrastructure, Grok, X), before covering the growth strategy, lunar ambitions and financial track record with segment revenue and capital expenditure. The closing section shows the revenue growth, margin and net income targets, and an appendix reconciles non-GAAP measures to GAAP. Slides worth studying include the market sizing bar chart on slide 13, the launch cost comparison on slide 24, the Starlink satellite and subscriber charts on slide 29, and the segment financials on slides 47 and 48. Many slides use large numerals and short headline claims, and a number of charts carry the argument directly.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the SpaceX roadshow presentation have?**

The SpaceX roadshow presentation for its proposed IPO, dated June 2026, has 60 pages. Slide 2 holds the forward-looking statements disclaimer and slide 3 holds the offering summary.

**What does the SpaceX deck say about its market size?**

Slide 13 states "WE ALREADY ADDRESS A MASSIVE $6T MARKET" and breaks the opportunity into SPACE: $370B, CONNECTIVITY: $1.6T and AI: $3.8T. Slide 14 then shows how AI unlocks an even larger opportunity.

**What is the SpaceX offering price and ticker symbol in the deck?**

Slide 3 lists the offering price as $135 per share and the ticker as SPCX on Nasdaq and Nasdaq Texas. The offering size is 555.6M shares (100% Primary), with an expected pricing date of June 11th, 2026.

**What payload capacity does the SpaceX deck give for its rocket fleet?**

Slide 18, titled "World's Only Fleet of Reusable Rockets," gives payload capacity to LEO of 23 Metric Tons for Falcon 9, 64 Metric Tons for Falcon Heavy and 100 Metric Tons for Starship V3. The slide notes that all payload capacity metrics reflect expected capacity.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed image slide with no grid. The SpaceX logo is centred horizontally at roughly 47 to 53 percent of slide height. The title sits left-aligned at the lower left, about 68 to 75 percent down, and the date sits directly beneath it, left-aligned at about 78 percent down. There is no footer, slide number or other furniture, and margins are about 3 percent on the left.

**Visuals**

Full-bleed photograph of Mars seen from orbit: a dark, nearly black sky fills the upper two-thirds, and a curved reddish-orange planetary limb runs from the lower left to the right edge at about 65 percent of slide height. The limb shows rust-brown and ochre terrain with darker crater-like shading and a thin pale-blue atmospheric glow along its edge. Lighting is a low sun from the left, so the surface is lit warmly and the lower third fades to dark grey-green. The image is not cropped and is not boxed, and no tint or overlay is applied beyond the image itself. Logo: the white SpaceX wordmark with the swoosh tail to its right, flat white with no shadow, sitting over the black sky above the planet limb.

**Design**

Background: Full-bleed photograph of Mars limb on a black #000000 field. Black full-bleed background with a dark Mars photograph covering the whole slide. The logo and title are pure white #FFFFFF. The date is light grey #C8C8C8. Title is a regular-weight geometric grotesque sans-serif in GS Sans style, letter-spaced, at about 7.5 percent of slide height. The date is the same family in regular weight at about 3.2 percent of slide height. No shapes, lines, borders, corner radii, shadows or gradients are present.

### Slide 4

**Text**

OUR MISSION
To build the systems and technologies necessary to make life multiplanetary, to understand the true nature of the universe, and to extend the light of consciousness to the stars
While we remain dedicated to our fundamental mission, our progress in accessing space continues to yield opportunities that enrich life on Earth. For example, by dramatically reducing the cost of access to space, we have been able to expand our mission to address some of the Earth's most pressing challenges, including bridging the digital divide by aiming to connect over three billion unconnected people to the internet and humanity's collective knowledge
The rapid emergence of the AI era intensifies the urgency of our mission, as AI has the potential to accelerate not only space exploration, but also transformative societal advancements on Earth. AI's ability to revolutionize human potential is directly dependent on meeting exponentially increasing resource demands

**Layout**

Full-bleed black slide with a left text column and a right-side image. **Visuals**

Photograph-style rendered image of two celestial bodies on black space. Left: a crescent Moon, lit from the right, roughly 41% to 57% of slide width and 36% to 80% of slide height, with a dark blue-black unlit side showing faint scattered bright specks, and a thin bright highlight line along its upper terminator. Right: Mars, orange-red and lit from the right, roughly 62% to 98% of slide width and 12% to 89% of slide height, with visible craters, a canyon system across its lower right, and a dark night side on its left with scattered warm glowing city-light specks and a bright cluster near its centre-left. Composition: the two bodies sit side by side with the Moon overlapping the lower left of the Mars disc region. The image is not cropped in a box; its black background blends into the slide, so it appears full-bleed in tone. Lighting: a single distant light source from the right. Also a white logo mark at bottom-left, a thin white swoosh-style X symbol, and the page number.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed image slide with no grid or columns. The title sits in the left third, vertically centred at about 40 to 55 percent of the slide height, left-aligned at about 3.9 percent from the left edge. The body paragraph sits directly below the title, left-aligned to the same edge, in three lines. A small logo sits in the bottom-left corner. No footer, slide number, or date is visible. The right half of the slide holds only the photograph.

**Visuals**

One full-bleed photograph of a vertical stainless-steel rocket vehicle (Starship-style upper stage with nose cone, two small fins at the top and two larger fins at the base, and a small rectangular flap on the right side) flying above the curved limb of Earth. The vehicle is centred at about x 49 percent, y 36 to 65 percent, viewed side-on, nose pointing up, and is small relative to the frame. The upper part of the frame is near-black space. A thin bright blue atmospheric band runs diagonally from the right edge at about y 36 percent down to the left at about y 45 percent. Below it, the Earth's surface shows blue-grey cloud layers over dark blue ocean, with the lower right lighter and the lower left darker. Lighting comes from the right, so the vehicle's right side and the horizon are brightest. Dominant colours are black #000000, deep navy #0B1F3A, steel grey #7A8594, and atmospheric blue #5B8FD0. The photo is uncropped and full-bleed, with a dark gradient overlay that darkens the left side to black so the white text is legible. Two 623 by 540 pixel gradient images cover the left 64.9 percent of the slide, and four 2 by 2 pixel images are placed in the left text area; these are effectively invisible tints. A small white logo (a stylised X with a swoosh) sits at the bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Full-bleed space photograph of Earth's limb and a rocket, with black #000000 in the upper area and a dark left-side gradient overlay. Black background #000000 with a full-bleed space photograph. Title text is white #FFFFFF in a geometric sans-serif (GS Sans-like), regular weight, all capitals, with slightly open letter-spacing, at about 7.8 percent of slide height for cap height and about 5.5 percent cap height. Body text is white #FFFFFF in the same family, regular weight, sentence case, at about 2.6 percent of slide height. There is no accent colour; the only colours come from the photograph. Lines are not drawn. Shapes have no corner radii, shadows, or borders. The left-side gradient overlay goes from black at the left edge to transparent toward the centre.

**Layout**

Three-column grid under a two-line all-caps title that spans nearly the full width, starting at the left margin of about 5% of slide width. Each column (left 5%, centre 35.8%, right 66.7%) has a bold header with a grey hairline beneath it, a subtitle, a description, then a stacked list of four label/value rows separated by grey hairlines; values are right-aligned to each column's right edge. A footnote sits bottom left beside the logo, and the page number sits bottom right; there is no visible footer rule.

**Visuals**

A full-bleed black-and-white photograph of the lunar surface fills the bottom 38.5% of the slide, with a curved limb horizon near its top edge and a crater-pocked grey surface below. A black gradient overlay fades the top of the photo into the solid black background. The photo is cropped by the slide edges, not boxed. Lighting is low, raking light from the left, with grey tones. Also present: one small white X-style logo at bottom left, thin grey hairline rules (15 in total: 3 under column headers, 3 under descriptions, and 9 between stat rows), and no charts, tables or other graphics.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 with a full-bleed grey lunar photograph across the bottom 38.5% of the slide, faded to black at its top edge. Pure black background with white all-caps geometric grotesque type in the GS Sans family (regular and medium weights for labels, light-to-regular for large figures). The title is about 8.3% of slide height in cap-height-driven size; column headers about 3.8%; subtitles and descriptions about 2.8%; stat labels about 1.5% in widely tracked caps; large figures about 6.6%. Titles, body and figures are white #FFFFFF; hairlines and rules are mid-grey #7F7F7F at about 1 px; footnote and page number are light grey #BFBFBF. No shadows, no corner radii, no rounded shapes; the only gradient is the black fade over the photo.

**Review notes**

**Text**

WE MAKE THE EXTRAORDINARY POSSIBLE
SPACE
The FIRST private company to develop and launch a liquid fuel rocket to reach orbit (2008)
The FIRST private company to successfully dock a spacecraft with the International Space Station (2012)
The FIRST to successfully propulsively land (2015) and refly orbital-class rocket boosters (2017)
The FIRST private company to transport astronauts to orbit and fly to and from the International Space Station (2020)
CONNECTIVITY
The FIRST to begin deploying a large-scale LEO broadband satellite constellation (2019)
The FIRST to manufacture consumer-grade phased-array user terminals at scale (2022)
The FIRST to deploy a large-scale LEO satellite-to-mobile constellation (2025)
The ONLY low-latency network available globally
AI
The FIRST to build a gigawatt-scale AI training cluster and largest coherent supercomputer (2026)
The FIRST gigawatt-scale Megapack battery installation (2026)
The ONLY company capable of building orbital AI compute at scale
7

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph with the title running across the top left at the top margin, and a single left-aligned text column starting about 27% from the left edge, containing three stacked sections (SPACE, CONNECTIVITY, AI), each with a heading, a thin horizontal rule and a list of four, four and three lines. The white logo sits bottom left and the slide number sits bottom right, both small and near the bottom margin; the left third of the slide is left open to show the rocket and tower.

**Visuals**

Full-bleed photograph of a tall grey-white rocket stage in flight at upper left, its engines firing orange flame and a thin white exhaust trail trailing below it, beside a dark steel launch tower with horizontal access arms (chopsticks) extending left from its upper section and a second arm lower left, against a large billowing orange-brown exhaust cloud on the left and a grey-blue sky on the right. Lighting is daylight with the scene slightly hazy; dominant colours are slate grey #5E6B76, rust orange #B9764A, pale sky blue-grey #8C9BA8 and near-black tower steel #222427. The image is cropped to the full slide with no border, and a black overlay at 30% opacity tints the whole picture to darken it. Two thin light-grey horizontal rules (#C8C8C8, about 0.2% of slide height, 1 px stroke) sit under the SPACE and CONNECTIVITY headings and a third sits under AI, each spanning the text column width.

**Design**

Background is a darkened photograph with a black 30% overlay, giving a muted grey-blue and orange cast. Title is white #FFFFFF in an all-caps geometric sans-serif regular weight with wide letter-spacing, about 7.5% of slide height. Section headings are white #FFFFFF in a bold sans-serif with capitals, about 3.8% of slide height. Body text is white #FFFFFF in a regular-weight humanist sans-serif, about 2.3% of slide height, with the words FIRST and ONLY set in bold white. Rules are light grey #C8C8C8 with a hairline stroke. No corner radii, shadows or gradients on shapes.

A bright white-orange glowing streak curves diagonally from top-centre down to the lower-right, suggesting exhaust or light bloom. Left two-thirds is a soft out-of-focus gradient of deep navy, indigo and teal fading to dark near the top-left corner. Lighting: warm orange-peach rim light on the central curve, cool blue ambient light elsewhere. Dominant colours: navy #0B1F3A, indigo #3B3B8C, peach #E8A277, slate grey #7A7F8C. Full-bleed, not cropped inside a box; a darkening tint is present over the left side to support the white text. No other visuals.

**Layout**

Full-bleed dark slide with a tall, vertically oriented photograph of a rocket stage in a hangar occupying the left 59% and fading into near-black on the right. The title sits top-left at about x 5 and y 7 to 14, spanning roughly two-thirds of the width. Three column headers (SPACE, CONNECTIVITY, AI) sit on one line at y 16 to 19, centred over their columns. A vertical timeline with five white dots and labels sits in the left-centre (dots at x 21.6), with five rows of centred descriptions in three columns to the right. Tagline sits bottom-right of centre at y 90, the logo bottom-left at x 4.4 and y 94, and the page number bottom-right at x 97 and y 96. Margins are about 5 percent left and 2 percent right.

**Visuals**

One photograph: a tall cylindrical stainless steel rocket stage (vertical, centred slightly left, roughly x 15 to 45) standing inside a dark industrial hangar with scaffolding, catwalks, gantry frames and angled lattice struts on both sides. Viewed from low, straight-on, full height, cropped top and bottom by the slide edge. Lighting is soft, diffuse and cool, with bright metal highlights on the stage and darker shadowed hangar structure. Dominant colours are grey, steel silver and near-black. The image is full-height and sits on the left 59 percent of the slide, with a black gradient veil over the right portion so it fades into #0D0D0D. Several black translucent bands (opacity 0.10) lie across each row. A vertical white timeline line runs from y 21 to y 88 at x 21.5, with five white filled circles (about 1.9 by 3.4 percent each) centred on it at the row heights, and a white stylised X-shaped logo mark bottom-left. No charts, tables or other graphics.

### Slide 10

**Text**

THE / ALGORITHM / 01 / MAKE THE REQUIREMENTS / LESS DUMB / 02 / DELETE THE PART OR / PROCESS STEP / 03 / OPTIMIZE / 04 / ACCELERATE / 05 / AUTOMATE / 10

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a black slide. The title THE ALGORITHM sits top-left at about 5% from the left edge. Below it, a numbered list of five rows runs in a left column about 5% to 33% of the width, separated by thin horizontal rules. A photograph fills the right column from about 39% to the right edge (full bleed on the right side), spanning roughly 22% to 90% of the height. The logo sits bottom-left and the page number bottom-right.

**Visuals**

One photograph of three rocket engine assemblies (Raptor-type) standing upright on white pallets on grey concrete, lined up left to right in front of a corrugated light grey metal wall. The left engine is unlabelled and has a weathered bronze-green nozzle skirt. The middle engine has a pale sage-green nozzle skirt with the number 569 painted in white on it, and a red-capped sensor on top. The right engine has a dark charcoal nozzle with the number 1 painted in white. Lighting is flat, diffuse daylight. Dominant colours are silver-grey, charcoal, weathered green and white. Composition is a horizontal eye-level frame with all three engines fully visible; the photo is not cropped and is boxed to the right edge with no overlay or tint. One small logo: a white stylised X-with-swoosh mark at bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 fill covering the whole slide. Black #000000 background throughout. Title text is white #FFFFFF in a light-weight geometric sans-serif (GS Sans Regular style), all capitals, large, about 7.6% of slide height per line. List numbers 01 to 05 are white #FFFFFF in a bold condensed sans-serif, about 6.3% of slide height. List labels are white #FFFFFF in regular-weight sans-serif capitals with wide letter-spacing, about 3.0% of slide height. Horizontal rules are mid grey #8C8C8C, about 0.15% of slide height thick. Page number is white #FFFFFF, small regular sans-serif. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 12

**Text**

OUR REPEATABLE\nBUSINESS MODEL\nSEVEN\nLeverage our unparalleled launch capabilities to enable massive scale\nSIX\nIdentify and create new trillion-dollar market opportunities\nFIVE\nDesign a solution with world-class engineering and first-principles thinking\nFOUR\nApply “The Algorithm” (make less dumb, delete, optimize, accelerate, automate)\nTHREE\nVertically integrate all the way to the end customer\nTWO\nContinuously drive cost down and throughput up\nONE\nGenerate significant cash flow and reinvest in the future\nLIFTOFF\n12

**Layout**

Two-zone split slide with no grid cards. The title sits top-left at about 4.4% from the left edge and 7.5% from the top, set in two stacked lines. Below it, a left column of seven ruled rows (number label in a left column, statement in a right column, both left-aligned) runs from about 29% to 81% of the height, followed by the word LIFTOFF on its own line. The right roughly 47% of the slide is a full-height night photograph that fades into the black left half. The SpaceX-style logo sits bottom-left and the slide number sits bottom-right, both in the margin.

**Visuals**

One photograph filling the right side (x 46.5% to 100%, full height, full-bleed to the top, bottom and right edges, with a black-to-transparent gradient fading its left edge into the black background). Subject: a tall vertical Falcon 9 first-stage booster standing upright on its four deployed landing legs on a dark ground, with the word SPACE painted vertically down its body in dark letters, a small Falcon 9 wordmark and a small American flag decal near the top, and scorched, soot-streaked panels along the lower body. What is happening: the booster is standing still, presumably after a landing. Composition: vertical subject centred at about x 75% of the slide, nearly filling the frame height, with the top of the booster near y 11% and the legs near y 95%. Lighting: a cool blue glow from a low horizon on the right side and warm light on the booster's lower metal sections; a starfield and faint clouds fill the dark blue sky. Dominant colours: near-black #05080F sky at the top, deep blue #1B4F78 to pale cyan #8FC4E0 at the horizon, booster off-white #D9CFC2 and dark grey #2A2A2A. The photo is not cropped at its sides, not boxed, and has no overlay other than the left-edge fade into black. Foreground: silhouetted dead shrubs and a dark flat horizon band across the bottom-left of the photo. Horizontal hairline rules (grey #8A8A8A, about 0.1% of slide height thick) separate the seven rows, spanning from x 4.4% to about x 58.8%. The logo is a white stylised X-like mark with a swoosh, at bottom-left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 across the whole slide, with the right-hand photograph overlaid. Black #000000 background with no texture. Titles are white #FFFFFF, in an all-caps geometric sans-serif (Good Sans style, regular weight, slight letter spacing), about 7.6% of slide height for the cap height of the title, i.e. roughly 7.6% em. Row labels (SEVEN, SIX, FIVE, FOUR, THREE, TWO, ONE) are bold, all caps, white #FFFFFF, about 2.8% em. Row statements are regular weight sentence case, white #FFFFFF, about 2.8% em. LIFTOFF is bold all caps white #FFFFFF, about 2.8% em. Accent colour: none apart from the blue photograph. Dividers are thin grey #8A8A8A rules, flat, no radius, no shadow. Slide number is regular, white, about 1.1% em. The photograph has a soft gradient fade on its left edge only.

### Slide 13

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A stacked market-size bar chart with labelled segment totals makes the addressable market argument visible at a glance.
- Review: Charts carry the argument directly, with headline claims as titles and big numerals (slides 13, 24, 29, 47 to 54).
- Worth keeping: Keep the headline-claim titles, large numerals, the stepped market bar on slide 13, the cost comparison on slide 24 with its percentage callouts, and the three-panel layout on slide 29.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed black slide with a left-aligned all-caps title across the top (about 6.7% from the top, starting at about 5% from the left margin), a waterfall chart occupying the middle band from about 30% to 74% of the height, a horizontal baseline at about 73.6%, category labels below it, and three grouped segment bars (SPACE, CONNECTIVITY, AI) at about 81% to 85% of the height. A small logo sits bottom-left at about 5% from the left and 93% from the top, and the slide number sits bottom-right at about 97% from the left and 96% from the top.

**Visuals**

Waterfall (bridge) chart with no axis titles, no gridlines and no numeric axes; values are printed above each bar. Chart title: none printed beyond the slide title. Bars from left to right, each with its value printed above: Space-Enabled Solutions $370 B (white #FFFFFF bar, about 7.7% from left, 8.7% wide, from the baseline up to about 70.9% of height); Starlink Broadband $870 B (light grey #C8C8C8 floating bar, 20.7% from left, 8.7% wide, top at 64.4%, height 6.5%); Starlink Mobile $740 B (light grey #C8C8C8 floating bar, 33.7% from left, top at 58.8%, height 5.6%); AI Infrastructure $2.4 T (mid grey #868686 floating bar, 46.7% from left, top at 40.6%, height 18.2%); Consumer Subscriptions $760 B (mid grey #868686 floating bar, 59.7% from left, top at 34.9%, height 5.7%); Digital Advertising $600 B (mid grey #868686 floating bar, 72.7% from left, top at 30.4%, height 4.5%); Near-Term Total Addressable Market $5.7 T (dark grey #525252 full bar, 85.7% from left, 8.7% wide, top at 30.4%, height 43.3%, extending down to the baseline). Each bar is a flat rectangle with square corners and no outline. Bars are stepped so each floating bar starts where the previous one ends. Below the chart, three segment bars with bold uppercase black labels: SPACE: $370B in a white #FFFFFF box (5.0% from left, 13.4% wide), CONNECTIVITY: $1.6T in a light grey #C8C8C8 box (18.6% from left, 26.2% wide), AI: $3.8T in a mid grey #868686 box (45.2% from left, 38.3% wide), all 4.3% tall at 80.9% from top with thin black gaps between them. The Space segment matches the white bar, the Connectivity segment spans the Starlink Broadband and Starlink Mobile bars, and the AI segment spans the AI Infrastructure, Consumer Subscriptions and Digital Advertising bars. Baseline: thin horizontal line in grey #868686, about 1 px wide, from about 5.5% to 96.5% at 73.6% from top. Logo: white stylised X/rocket-swoosh mark (SpaceX-style logo) at bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 fill covering the entire slide. Pure black background with white and grey graphics in a monochrome palette. Title: all-caps, regular-weight geometric sans-serif (DIN-like), white #FFFFFF, roughly 8.3% of slide height em (cap height about 5.8% of height), slight letter-spacing, single line. Value labels and category labels: light-grey #C8C8C8 regular-weight sans-serif (DIN-like), value labels about 3.2% em, category labels about 2.3% em, centred over bars. Segment labels: bold uppercase condensed sans-serif in black #000000 on the coloured boxes, about 2.0% em. Slide number: small light-grey sans-serif about 1.5% em. Shapes have hard square edges with no corner radius, shadows or gradients. The baseline is the only line. No gradients anywhere.

### Slide 14

**Text**

AI UNLOCKS AN EVEN LARGER OPPORTUNITY / $370B / $870B / $740B / $2.4T / $760B / $600B / $22.7T / $28.5T / Space-Enabled / Solutions / Starlink / Broadband / Starlink / Mobile / AI / Infrastructure / Consumer / Subscriptions / Digital / Advertising / Enterprise / Applications / Total / Addressable / Market / SPACE: $370B / CONNECTIVITY: $1.6T / AI: $26.5T / 14

**Layout**

Title sits top-left, left-aligned at about a 5% left margin and roughly 6% from the top. A waterfall-style column chart fills the middle and lower slide, with bars sitting on a thin horizontal baseline at about 70.8% of slide height; category labels sit under the baseline, and three horizontal segment bands with bold labels sit below them at about 81% to 85%. The SpaceX-style logo is bottom-left and the slide number is bottom-right, both on the margin.

**Visuals**

Vertical bar chart (waterfall-style, floating bars) with no axes, gridlines or legend titles. Seven bars step up from left to right, each bar labelled above with its value: Space-Enabled Solutions $370B (very short bar), Starlink Broadband $870B, Starlink Mobile $740B, AI Infrastructure $2.4T, Consumer Subscriptions $760B, Digital Advertising $600B (all five short, thin grey bars about 0.5% to 3% of slide height tall), Enterprise Applications $22.7T (tall light grey bar from about 31.6% to 63.7% of slide height), and Total Addressable Market $28.5T (tallest dark grey bar from about 31.6% to 70.8% of slide height, flush with the baseline). Values are printed for every bar, so no estimates are needed. Below the chart are three segment bands: a white box labelled SPACE: $370B spanning the first bar, a light grey box labelled CONNECTIVITY: $1.6T spanning the Starlink Broadband and Starlink Mobile bars, and a mid grey box labelled AI: $26.5T spanning AI Infrastructure through Enterprise Applications. The chart area sits on a translucent black panel (opacity 0.70). Seven 2 by 2 pixel placeholder images are present but are invisible flat fills. The logo is a white stylised rocket-swoosh mark.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 full-bleed background. Pure black background with white and grey elements, a monochrome palette. Title in white #FFFFFF, uppercase, a regular-weight geometric sans-serif (DIN-like or Eurostile-like) with wide letter-spacing, about 8.3% of slide height. Bar value labels in mid grey #7A7A7A, condensed sans-serif regular, about 3.2% of slide height. Category labels in dark grey #7F7F7F for the first six bars and white #FFFFFF for Enterprise Applications and Total Addressable Market, condensed sans-serif regular, about 2.5% of slide height. Segment band labels in black #000000, bold condensed sans-serif, about 2.3% of slide height. Bars: #FFFFFF is not used for bars; small bars #2A2A2A to #3A3A3A, Enterprise bar #8A8A8A, TAM bar #404040. Segment bands: #FFFFFF, #C8C8C8 and #868686. Baseline is a 1px line in #555555. Shapes are sharp-cornered rectangles with no shadows or gradients. Slide number about 1.2% of slide height in white.

**Layout**

Single full-slide layout with the title flush left at the top, a vertical timeline on the left of the content area, and 11 rows of date, marker, item and description running down the slide. Each row has a date right-aligned at about 8 percent of width, a dot on the timeline, an item label or logo at about 12 percent, and a description left-aligned at 34 percent; hairline rules separate rows. The footer holds the small logo and source line at bottom left and the page number at bottom right, with margins of about 4 to 5 percent.

**Visuals**

Vertical grey timeline line running from about 19 percent to 91 percent of slide height at about 10.5 percent of width, with 11 small grey filled circles centred on it, one per row. Eleven horizontal hairline grey rules, one below each row, spanning from about 11.6 percent to 96.9 percent of width. Four logos: the white xAI X-mark logo in row 1 (about 12.6 to 14.3 percent wide, 21.4 to 24.7 percent high); the grey and white Cursor cube logo with CURSOR wordmark in row 6 (about 12.8 to 19.9 percent wide); the American Airlines logo, black wordmark with white swoosh, in row 10 (about 11.9 to 20 percent wide, 79.4 to 84.7 percent high); and the white SpaceX-style X logo at bottom left (about 5 to 8.9 percent wide, 93 to 95.8 percent high). No photographs, charts or tables.

**Layout**

Five equal-width columns sit in a single row below a full-width title, separated by four thin vertical hairlines that run from about 31.7% to 78.3% of the slide height. Each column stacks a bold all-caps header, a large date or figure, a two-line caption, a second large figure and its caption, all left-aligned to a common left edge per column. The title is left-aligned at top left (x 4.4%, y 6.5%) with a left margin of about 4.4%; the SpaceX-style logo sits bottom left and the slide number '16' sits bottom right, with a footer-level margin of about 3% from the bottom edge.

**Visuals**

Four thin vertical divider lines, each light grey about #8C8C8C at roughly 1 px width, spaced at x about 22.6%, 40.9%, 59.1% and 77.3% of slide width, each spanning y about 31.7% to 78.3%. A small white logo mark at bottom left (a stylised swept-back X/arrow glyph). No photographs, charts, or illustrations.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 fill covering the whole slide. Pure black background (#000000) with all text in white (#FFFFFF); no gradients, shadows, or tints. Title is a light-to-regular weight geometric grotesque sans-serif (GS Sans style), all caps, roughly 8.3% of slide height em. Column headers are a bold condensed all-caps grotesque sans-serif, about 4.6% of slide height em, set in two lines with tight leading. Large figures are a regular-weight grotesque sans-serif, all caps where letters appear, about 5.3% of slide height em. Captions are a regular-weight grotesque sans-serif, sentence case, about 2.8% of slide height em, with tight leading. Divider lines are hairline, no corner radii.

### Slide 17

**Text**

OUR SPACE
BUSINESS
We are the world's leading launch provider, with more than 80% of mass to orbit for the world since 2023
This critical capability is our foundation and enables the rest of our businesses

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph fills the whole slide. A three-block text stack sits left-aligned on the left third at a 3.8% left margin: a two-line all-caps title at about 40% to 55% of the slide height, then a two-paragraph body from about 61% to 78%. There is no visible footer, slide number or header; a small white logo sits bottom-left at about 94% to 97% height.

**Visuals**

Photograph, full-bleed: a view of Earth from orbit, with the blue-and-white curved limb of the planet filling the upper two-thirds and the right side. Clouds are white over dark blue ocean and land. In the foreground, a red sports car (a Tesla Roadster) runs diagonally from lower-left to lower-right, showing its glossy red bodywork and a black roof panel at about mid-left. A mannequin in a white spacesuit sits in the driver seat, with a grey-and-white helmet and gloved arm resting on the door, seen from behind and to the right. A small red light glows on the car's dashboard near the centre-left. Composition is wide-angle with a fisheye-like curvature. Lighting is from the sunlit Earth above and right; the car is in shadow with red reflected highlights along its lower edge. Dominant colours are black #000000, deep blue #1E3A6E, white #FFFFFF and red #B3201C. The image is not cropped in a boxed frame; it runs to all four edges. A black gradient overlay covers the left third, fading from solid black at the left edge to transparent around the centre, to make the text legible. No chart, table or diagram.

**Design**

Background black #000000 with the photograph over it. Title text white #FFFFFF, geometric sans-serif (GS Sans style, close to DIN-like or Eurostile-free grotesque), regular weight, all capitals, wide letter-spacing, about 7.9% of slide height per em, so caps about 5.5% of height. Body text white #FFFFFF, same sans family, regular weight, sentence case, about 2.6% of slide height per em, with about 1.2 line spacing. Paragraphs separated by one blank line. Accent colour: the red of the car #B3201C. No shapes, borders, corner radii or shadows apart from the gradient overlay. Logo is a small white stylised X-shaped mark in white #FFFFFF.

### Slide 18

**Text**

WORLD'S ONLY FLEET OF REUSABLE ROCKETS
FALCON 9
FALCON HEAVY
STARSHIP V3
PAYLOAD CAPACITY TO LEO
23 Metric Tons
64 Metric Tons
100 Metric Tons
TOTAL NUMBER OF FLIGHTS
~620
11
12
SUCCESS RATES
99%
100%
Testing Phase
USE CASES
Starlink V2 Mini Satellite, Cargo and Humans to
Earth's Orbit, and Deep Space Missions
Heavy Cargo to Earth's Orbit
Deep Space Missions
Starlink V3 Satellites, Starlink Mobile V2 Satellites,
AI Compute Satellites, Interplanetary Travel
Note: Information as of March 31, 2026 except for Starship flights which is inclusive of flight 12. Number of flights since inception of each vehicle. Falcon 9 payload to LEO reflects fully expendable configuration. All payload
capacity metrics reflect expected capacity.
18

**Layout**

Single-slide table layout on a full black field: a wide title runs across the top from the left margin at about 5% of the slide width, with the top of the caps near 8% of the slide height. Three vehicle columns (Falcon 9, Falcon Heavy, Starship V3) are centred at roughly 33%, 58% and 82% of slide width, with the row labels in a left column starting at the 5% margin. Four horizontal hairline rules span the table from 5% to 94.5% width beneath the vehicle names, separating the payload, flights, success rate and use-case rows. A footer note sits at the bottom left beside the SpaceX logo, and the page number sits at bottom right near the right margin.

**Visuals**

Three vehicle illustrations, all rendered in white and light-grey metallic tones on black with no boxes, frames or overlays. Falcon 9: two vertical rockets side by side, a booster on the left with a white nose cone, a black band on the lower body and small red markings, and a second-stage-like vehicle on the right with a dark grey nose and black band; both show small blue-grey SpaceX lettering on the body and black landing legs at the base. Falcon Heavy: a central vertical core with a white nose cone, flanked by two side boosters with white nose cones and black engine bases, all standing on a common black base area. Starship V3: a tall silver stainless-steel stack with a pointed nose and grey delta flaps near the top, a thin band of flaps mid-body, and a dark engine section at the base. The three images are standing vehicles drawn at the same baseline scale in full profile, with the Starship roughly twice the height of the others. Table: three columns and four rows. Row 1 PAYLOAD CAPACITY TO LEO: 23 Metric Tons, 64 Metric Tons, 100 Metric Tons. Row 2 TOTAL NUMBER OF FLIGHTS: ~620, 11, 12. Row 3 SUCCESS RATES: 99%, 100%, Testing Phase. Row 4 USE CASES: Starlink V2 Mini Satellite, Cargo and Humans to Earth's Orbit, and Deep Space Missions; Heavy Cargo to Earth's Orbit Deep Space Missions; Starlink V3 Satellites, Starlink Mobile V2 Satellites, AI Compute Satellites, Interplanetary Travel. Hairline rules in dark grey separate rows. A small SpaceX logo mark sits at bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 fill covering the whole slide. Pure black background (#000000) with white (#FFFFFF) title and body text, and light grey (#D0D0D0) for the footnote and page number. Accent colour: none apart from grey hairlines (#5A5A5A) and the metallic greys of the vehicles. Typeface: a geometric grotesque sans-serif similar to GSSans, regular weight for the title and the row labels and values, bold for the three vehicle names. Title is all capitals, about 7.5% of slide height (cap height about 5.4%), with slight letter-spacing. Row labels and values are about 2.3% of slide height, all caps for labels and sentence case for values. Footnote about 0.9% of slide height. Hairlines are 1 px thin, no corner radii, no shadows, no gradients.

### Slide 20

**Text**

THE VALUE OF ROCKET REUSABILITY / COST / Reuse of expensive hardware / lowering launch costs / LAUNCH CADENCE / Enables higher flight rate, rapid large-scale / deployments and human transportation / RELIABILITY / Post-flight inspections feed an iterative re- / engineering loop that increases reliability / ENVIRONMENT / Reduces manufacturing waste, emissions and / ocean-polluting debris / ADVANTAGE TO SPACEX / LOWER / Cost per launch compared to / expendable rockets / MOST / Launches of any provider / in the industry / ADVANTAGE TO OUR CUSTOMERS / LOWEST / Cost per kg to orbit / LOWER / Insurance premiums / 20

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph with a two-column text arrangement over it. The title runs across the top left at about 5% margin, left column holds four stacked blocks (COST, LAUNCH CADENCE, RELIABILITY, ENVIRONMENT) left-aligned from x 5.3%, and the right column holds two sections (ADVANTAGE TO SPACEX above, ADVANTAGE TO OUR CUSTOMERS below) with centred headings and pairs of centred figures. The rocket sits in the centre of the slide between the two columns. A small logo sits bottom left and the slide number bottom right.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full-bleed across the whole slide, of a vertical SpaceX Falcon 9 booster standing on its landing legs at night. Subject: the first-stage booster, white-grey body with a dark scorched lower section and black landing-leg struts splayed to the ground; a US flag decal and small 'FALCON 9' marking are visible near the upper third, and 'SPACEX' is printed vertically in black along the body. Composition: rocket centred, running from the top edge to the bottom edge, roughly x 47% to 53%, with the sky filling both sides. Framing is full-height and the photo is not cropped at the rocket. Lighting: night scene, the booster lit from below by a soft floodlight, with a cyan glow along the horizon. Dominant colours: deep navy #0A1A2F at top, blue #1E6FA0 and cyan #3FA9D6 near the horizon, white-grey #C9CED4 and brown-grey #5A4A40 on the rocket. Overlay: none visible; bare tree silhouettes along the bottom edge and a dark light pole near x 80% at the bottom right. Stars are scattered across the sky.

**Design**

Background is a night-sky photograph with no tint or gradient overlay beyond the natural sky gradient. Titles and headings are white #FFFFFF in a bold geometric sans (DIN-style), uppercase. The main title is in a regular-weight geometric sans (DIN-style), uppercase, white #FFFFFF, about 7.5% of slide height. Section headings are bold uppercase, about 5.2% of slide height. Body text is regular-weight DIN-style sans, white #FFFFFF, about 2.8% of slide height. Thin horizontal rules in pale grey-blue #8DA2B8 about 0.2% of slide height sit under each heading. No shapes, corner radii or shadows.

### Slide 21

**Text**

STARSHIP / First ever heavy-lift rocket built for full and rapid reusability / 12 test flights to date / Demonstrated successful atmospheric entry and precision landing / Proven Starship booster reusability; the remaining technical milestone is the catch and re-flight of the ship / Proven full-duration ascents and hot staging separation / Propellant transfer for missions beyond Earth's orbit / Robust multi-destination landing capability including Mechazilla tower system / Goal is to replicate airline-like operations with propellant (methane / liquid oxygen) to be the only marginal cost for launch / STARSHIP / SUPER HEAVY / Note: All references to tons in this presentation refer to metric tons. / 21

**Layout**

Dark full-bleed slide split into two columns: a left text column (about 4% to 48% of width) and a right photo panel (about 54% to 100% of width) with a dark gradient fading into the photo's left edge. The title STARSHIP sits top-left at about 6.7% from the top with a left margin of about 5%. The eight bullet items stack vertically in the left column, each separated by thin grey horizontal rules; the SpaceX logo sits bottom-left with the note beside it, and the page number sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Full-height vertical photograph of a Starship upper stage (black nose cone and payload section with a white/steel midsection and dark grid fins) stacked on a Super Heavy booster (white steel tanks with a dark lower section) on a tan-and-grey launch mount, shown as a tall narrow column in the right panel. Composition: the rocket runs the full height of the slide, with the nose tip cropped at the top edge and the base at the bottom edge; the surrounding sky is pale blue-grey fading to light grey toward the top right. Lighting is soft natural daylight with the metal lightly reflective. Dominant colours: black, steel grey, off-white and tan. The photo is full-bleed to the right and top and bottom edges, with a black-to-transparent gradient overlay across its left edge (about 54% to 60% of width). Two white callout labels, STARSHIP and SUPER HEAVY, each have a thin white horizontal leader line ending in a small solid white dot placed toward the rocket. Eight thin grey horizontal rules divide the bullet items in the left column. A small white SpaceX logo sits bottom-left.

**Design**

Background black #000000. Titles and bullet text white #FFFFFF; the note and page number are light grey #C8C8C8; callout labels white #FFFFFF; rules mid grey #7A7A7A at about 1 px; leader lines white about 2 px with white dots about 1.1% of slide width. Title STARSHIP is a light-weight geometric grotesque sans-serif in capitals, about 7.5% of slide height. Bullet text is a regular-weight humanist grotesque sans-serif, about 2.3% of slide height, with generous line spacing (about 3.1% between lines of a wrapped item). Callout labels are a bold condensed grotesque sans-serif in capitals, about 5% of slide height. Note text is a regular sans-serif about 1.1% of slide height. No rounded corners, shadows or gradients other than the photo fade.

**Layout**

Split composition on a 16:9 slide. The left 42% is a black field holding the title across the top, a bold subtitle with a unit line, and a white bar chart on a 20% to 90% axis. The right 58% is a full-bleed photograph that fades into black at its left edge, running behind the title's right end. Title left edge sits at 5% of width with its cap top at about 7.5% of height. Chart left margin about 5%. Source line and logo sit in a footer band at about 94% to 97% of height; page number '22' sits bottom right at 97% of width, same baseline as the source line.

**Visuals**

Vertical bar chart. Title: MASS TO ORBIT FOR THE WORLD. Subtitle: % SPACEX SHARE. Y axis: percentage share, labelled 20% to 90% in 10% steps, gridlines every 10%; the axis starts at 20%, not zero. X axis: categories 2021, 2022, 2023-2025 (no unit label). Series: one series, white bars. Labelled values: 2021 = 45%; 2022 = 65%; 2023-2025 = 80%+ (bar top about 82%, estimated, label printed as 80%+). No legend. No highlighted points; all bars same white. Photograph, right side: a Starship launch-tower scene at sunset. A tall stainless booster is held between the open arms of a dark steel lattice launch and catch tower, with engine flame visible below it at the top arm and a second bright exhaust plume below it. Tower is a dark grey-black steel lattice rising from bottom centre-right of the photo to the top. Sky is pale blue at top fading to orange and pink near a horizon at about 66% of height; sea sits at mid-right with a flat horizon; green-brown coastal land runs along the bottom. Lighting is low, warm sunset from the right. Composition is a vertical tower in the right third with the booster in the upper third. Photo is full-bleed to the right edge and top and bottom, not boxed or cropped to a shape. Left edge of the photo fades into the black field via a gradient from black at about 42% of width to transparent at about 52%. Logo: a small white SpaceX-style swoosh mark at bottom left.

**Design**

Background solid black #000000 with a fade into the photo at the right. Title: white #FFFFFF, uppercase, regular-weight geometric grotesque sans (close to GSSans), wide letter spacing, cap height about 5.8% of slide height. Subtitle: white #FFFFFF, uppercase bold condensed grotesque sans (DIN-like), cap height about 2.5% of height. Unit line: white #FFFFFF, uppercase regular grotesque sans, cap height about 1.8%. Axis labels and value labels: white #FFFFFF regular grotesque sans, digit height about 1.6% to 2.0% of height. Bars: solid white #FFFFFF, square corners, no outline, no shadow. Gridlines: thin light grey #BFBFBF, about 1 px, no dash, spanning the plot width. Footer text: light grey #BFBFBF regular sans, small. No shadows, no rounded corners, no decorative rules.

**Layout**

Two-zone layout: a left column (about x 5 to 57) holds the title, subtitle and bar-and-line chart, and a right zone (about x 38 to 100) is a full-bleed rocket photograph with the three-line callout placed over its darker left-hand area at about x 57 to 88. The title sits top-left at about 5% margin, left-aligned, in one line spanning about 71% of the width; the subtitle pair sits directly below it, left-aligned to the same margin. The SpaceX logo sits bottom-left at about 4% margin and the page number 23 sits bottom-right at about 3% margin.

**Visuals**

Chart: combined bar and line chart titled BOOSTER REUSABILITY ENABLES INCREASING LAUNCH RATES, with subtitle ANNUAL NUMBER OF FALCON FAMILY LAUNCHES BY SPACEX. Y axis: annual number of launches, unit count, range 0 to 180 in steps of 20, with horizontal light-grey gridlines at each step. X axis: years 2010, a break marked by two diagonal slashes, then 2017 to 2025 in single years; the 2010 bar is shown separately from the 2017 to 2025 run. Series 1, MISSIONS, drawn as a smooth white line with no markers: about 18 in 2017, about 21 in 2018, about 13 in 2019, about 27 in 2020, about 28 in 2021, about 30 in 2022 to 2023 region rising steeply, labelled 165 at the 2025 end point; values other than 165 are estimates. Series 2, NEW BOOSTERS FLOWN, drawn as solid grey #7F7F7F vertical bars: 2010 about 2, 2017 about 13, 2018 about 10, 2019 about 7, 2020 about 5, 2021 about 2, 2022 about 7, 2023 about 9, 2024 about 9, 2025 labelled 8. Data labels: 165 in bold white above the line end point at 2025; 8 in bold white above the 2025 bar. Legend: centred below the chart at about y 90%, a short white line swatch labelled MISSIONS followed by a grey square swatch labelled NEW BOOSTERS FLOWN. No highlighted points beyond the two labelled values. Photograph: a SpaceX Falcon family rocket launching vertically from the right of centre, with its white-hot exhaust plume trailing down beneath it; the booster body is dark grey and the vehicle is seen in silhouette against a very large pale-white sun low in the sky. Lighting is backlit from the sun, giving an orange and white glow; the sky is a warm orange gradient. Foreground shows dark silhouetted trees, a lattice launch tower on the far right, and two thin utility poles on the left. Framing: full-bleed, tall vertical composition, rocket centred at about x 79 of the slide; not boxed, not cropped at the edges apart from the bleed. Dominant colours: orange #E8731A, white #FFF6E0, black #000000 in the foreground. Overlay: the left edge of the photograph fades into solid black, creating a dark gradient that merges with the black slide background behind the chart. Logo: the white SpaceX logo mark (stylised swoosh) at bottom left, the only logo.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 background, with a full-bleed photograph occupying the right side of the slide. Black full-slide background with a sans-serif look similar to DIN or GS Sans, all-caps throughout, regular weight for the title and callouts, bold for the chart heading. Title is white #FFFFFF, very large, about 7.9% of slide height. Chart heading is bold white #FFFFFF at about 3.4% of slide height. Subtitle and axis labels are light white #FFFFFF regular at about 2.0 to 2.4% of slide height. Callout text is white #FFFFFF regular at about 2.9% of slide height, separated by line spacing of about 4.5% of slide height. Accents: grey bars #7F7F7F, white line #FFFFFF, vertical divider line #FFFFFF about 0.1% wide. Gridlines are thin mid-grey #5A5A5A at about 0.1% line weight. Page number is light grey #BFBFBF. No corner radii, no shadows; flat fills; photograph has a left-edge black gradient fade.

### Slide 24

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A cost-per-kilogram bar chart with percentage reduction callouts shows the launch cost story in one view.
- Review: Charts carry the argument directly, with headline claims as titles and big numerals (slides 13, 24, 29, 47 to 54).
- Worth keeping: Keep the headline-claim titles, large numerals, the stepped market bar on slide 13, the cost comparison on slide 24 with its percentage callouts, and the three-panel layout on slide 29.

**Text**

UNPARALLELED COST EFFICIENCY / STARSHIP: STEP FUNCTION IMPROVEMENT IN REUSABILITY / LAUNCH COST PER KG TO LOW EARTH ORBIT / $ 20,000 / $ 18,500 / $ 15,000 / $ 10,000 / 85% / Average Cost / Reduction / 92% / Average Cost / Reduction / 99%+ / Target Cost / Reduction / $ 5,000 / $ 2,700 / $ 1,400 / $ 0 / AVERAGE LAUNCH / COST (1970 – 2000) / FALCON 9 / FALCON HEAVY / STARSHIP / Source: NASA / 24

**Layout**

Full-bleed black slide with a single left-aligned column: the title sits top-left at about 6.5% from the top and 5% from the left edge, followed by a bold subtitle and a regular subtitle, then a bar chart occupying the left half from about 33% to 86% of the height. A vertical Starship launch photograph fills the right third and bleeds off the top and bottom edges. A small logo and source line sit at the bottom left and the page number sits at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

Bar chart titled LAUNCH COST PER KG TO LOW EARTH ORBIT (subtitle above the chart). Vertical bar chart, single series of launch cost in US dollars per kilogram to low Earth orbit. Y axis: $0 to $20,000, labelled every $5,000 ($ 0, $ 5,000, $ 10,000, $ 15,000, $ 20,000), with thin white horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X axis categories from left to right: AVERAGE LAUNCH COST (1970 – 2000); FALCON 9; FALCON HEAVY; STARSHIP. Values printed above bars: average launch cost $ 18,500 (grey bar, #7F7F7F); Falcon 9 $ 2,700 (white bar); Falcon Heavy $ 1,400 (white bar); Starship has no printed value, its bar is a thin white sliver of about $200 (estimate). No legend. No highlighted points beyond the grey bar. Above the Falcon 9, Falcon Heavy and Starship columns, three large percentage callouts: 85% Average Cost Reduction (over Falcon 9), 92% Average Cost Reduction (over Falcon Heavy), 99%+ Target Cost Reduction (over Starship). Photograph: a Starship vehicle flying vertically upward from a steel launch tower, with a bright orange-pink exhaust plume beneath it and dark smoke clouds rising at the bottom right. The vehicle is a tall grey-white rocket with a pointed nose and fins, centred around x 84% of the slide. Composition is vertical and full height, cropped at the top and bottom edges and blended into the black background on the left through a gradient. Lighting is dusky, with the sky dark grey to near black at the top and warm orange light from the exhaust. Dominant colours are grey, black, orange and pink. Logo: the white stylised X mark of SpaceX at the bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 with a Starship launch photograph blending in from the right. Black background (#000000) throughout. Titles are white (#FFFFFF). Body text is white (#FFFFFF) and labels are light grey. Accent: grey bar #7F7F7F against white bars #FFFFFF. Main title is a regular-weight, widely tracked sans-serif (GS Sans-like), all caps, about 8.3% of slide height for cap height and about 5.8% cap height. Subtitle is a bold condensed-style sans-serif, all caps, about 3.9% of slide height. Second subtitle and chart labels are a regular sans-serif, all caps, about 2.5% of slide height. Percentage callouts are large regular sans-serif numerals about 5% of slide height. Chart bars are flat rectangles with no border. Gridlines are thin white hairlines. The photograph has a dark gradient fading from left (black) to right (visible image). No corner radii, no shadows.

25

**Layout**

A full-height photograph occupies the left half (about 0 to 49% of width) and fades into black toward its right edge, while the chart sits on the black right half in a column starting at about 53% of width. The title is top-left at about 5% margin and the footnote runs along the bottom-left beside a small logo, with the page number bottom-right at about 97%.

**Visuals**

Photograph: a Starship booster during launch, shown from below in a near-vertical framing with the rocket's grid of about 33 engine bells in a dense circular cluster, glowing white-hot, at the upper-middle of the frame, surrounded by billowing tan and brown smoke and a pinkish-violet exhaust plume descending to the bottom edge. Lighting is self-lit by the engine flames against a dark blue-grey night sky at the left; dominant colours are slate blue-grey, warm cream-white, amber-brown and lilac-pink. The photo is full-height and cropped at the top and bottom edges, not boxed, and fades to black on its right side. Bar chart: vertical bar chart titled STARSHIP: THE KEY TO UNLOCKING NEW MARKETS with subtitle PAYLOAD CAPACITY (METRIC TONS). Y-axis runs 0 to 200 metric tons with labelled gridlines at 0, 50, 100, 150 and 200. Series values, labelled above each bar: Falcon 9 (expendable) 23; Falcon Heavy (expendable) 64; Starship V3 (reusable) 100; Future Starship (reusable) 200. Bars are solid white with no outlines, evenly spaced, categories labelled below in white uppercase. No legend; no highlighted points. Logo: a small white stylised X swoosh at bottom-left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 with the photograph fading into it on the left half. Black #000000 background with white #FFFFFF titles, body text, bars and gridlines; the photograph supplies all colour. Title is a light-to-regular geometric sans-serif (GS Sans style), all caps, wide letter-spacing, about 7.8% of slide height. Chart heading is a bold condensed-leaning geometric sans, all caps, about 3.5% of slide height. Subtitle and axis labels are regular-weight sans, about 2.2% of slide height; bar value labels about 3% of slide height; category labels about 3% of slide height in two lines. Footnote is regular sans about 1% of slide height in white. Lines are thin white hairlines about 0.1% of slide height; bars are flat rectangles with square corners, no shadows, no gradients except the photo fade.

**Text**

OUR CONNECTIVITY
BUSINESS
We leveraged our foundational launch capabilities to develop the
most advanced global satellite internet constellation

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph fills the slide edge to edge, with a dark gradient darkening the left side. A left-aligned text column starts at about 3.8% from the left edge: the all-caps title sits in the upper half of the column at about 41% from the top, with two body paragraphs below it at about 62% to 93% of the height. The photographed subject sits in the right half. No footer and no slide number.

**Visuals**

Photograph, full-bleed, uncropped to the slide frame (1428 by 802 px source). Subject: a man in a red hooded jacket with a grey backpack and headlamp, crouched on a rocky outcrop at a mountain summit, holding an open white tablet with a blank lit screen angled toward the viewer, with brown trousers and brown hiking boots. Behind him: layered mountain ridges with snow-dusted peaks, a long blue lake in a valley, and green forested slopes. Sky: dusk gradient from blue-violet at the top through pink to a pale blue band at the horizon. Lighting: soft low-angle dusk light, with the figure lit from the front. Dominant colours: red, pink, violet, blue, green and brown. Framing: subject placed in the lower right third, camera slightly above him. A black gradient overlay fades from opaque at the left edge to transparent at about 47% width, darkening the left side for the text. One small white logo mark at the bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Full-bleed photograph of a mountain summit at dusk, with a black gradient fading in from the left edge (base colour #000000). Full-bleed photographic background with a black-to-transparent gradient on the left. Title colour white #FFFFFF; body text off-white #F2F2F2; logo white #FFFFFF. Typeface: GS Sans (a geometric humanist sans-serif, recognised from the GSSans-Regular measurement). Title is all caps, regular weight, wide letter-spacing, about 7.6% of slide height per line (cap height about 5.3%). Body is regular weight, sentence case, about 2.4% of slide height, line spacing about 3.1% of slide height. Paragraphs are separated by one blank line. No corner radii, shadows or shapes visible; the gradient is the only overlay.

**Review notes**

- Review: Weak spots are some text over photos with low contrast (slides 7, 31, 42, 43), a few tiny captions and labels (slides 6, 27, 36, 52 to 54), and a few busy slides (9, 29, 36).

**Text**

UNRIVALED SATELLITE CONSTELLATION
V2 SATELLITES
V3 SATELLITES
COMPETITIVE STRENGTHS
Availability
Today
H2 2026
Bandwidth per Satellite
96 Gbps
1,024 Gbps
Deployment per Launch
27 Satellites
On Falcon 9
60 Satellites
On Starship
Bandwidth per Launch
2,600 Gbps
61,000 Gbps
MORE THAN 20X BANDWIDTH PER LAUNCH WITH V3
Unparalleled launch capabilities and rocket reusability led to the development of Starlink, making satellite internet economically viable
Integrated platform spanning architecture, chip design, software, power systems, and final assembly
Ability to scale new businesses leveraging the core satellite technology platform: Starlink Broadband, Starlink Mobile, and AI Compute
Note: Gbps are gigabits per second.  V3 satellites are in development.
27

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph behind all content with the title across the top left at about 7.5% from the top and a left margin of about 4.5%. Below the title a left-hand table occupies roughly x 4.7% to 58.7% with column headers at y 36%, four ruled rows and a right-aligned callout at the bottom; a right-hand column starting at x 69% holds the 'COMPETITIVE STRENGTHS' heading and three left-aligned paragraphs. The footer has the logo at bottom left, the note beside it and the page number '27' at bottom right at about x 97%.

**Visuals**

Full-bleed photograph of stacked satellite units: on the left and centre a dark, densely packed array of rectangular satellite panels with metallic edges, faint highlights and cable-like lines, diagonally receding toward the top right; on the right the curved limb of Earth with a thin blue atmospheric glow, white and grey cloud banks and brown-grey landmass in the lower right. Lighting is a dim, low-key cool light on the satellites with the Earth lit from the upper right. The image covers the whole slide with no crop frame and a dark black overlay or gradient that darkens the left side and the lower half, so text stays legible. Dominant colours are black, charcoal grey, deep blue and muted brown. Table: columns are a row-label column, V2 SATELLITES and V3 SATELLITES; rows are Availability (V2 Today, V3 H2 2026), Bandwidth per Satellite (V2 96 Gbps, V3 1,024 Gbps), Deployment per Launch (V2 27 Satellites, sub-line On Falcon 9; V3 60 Satellites, sub-line On Starship), Bandwidth per Launch (V2 2,600 Gbps, V3 61,000 Gbps). Thin grey horizontal rules separate the header from the first row and each row from the next, with a final rule under the last row. No chart. Logo: a small white swoosh-style X mark at bottom left.

**Design**

Background is black #000000 with a darkened photograph. Title is set in uppercase GSSans-Regular, a light geometric sans with normal weight and slight letter spacing, colour light grey #E8E8E8, title cap height about 5.8% of slide height (font size about 8.2% of height). Table column headers and row labels are bold uppercase or bold sentence-case sans in white #FFFFFF at about 3.3% of height. Table values are regular sans in light grey #E8E8E8 at about 3.3% of height, sub-lines are italic at about 2.2%. Competitive Strengths heading is bold uppercase white #FFFFFF at about 3.6%. Body paragraphs are regular sans with open letter spacing in #D9D9D9 at about 2.6% of height with line spacing about 3.4% of height. Footnote is about 1.0% in #BFBFBF. Rules are 1 px thin mid-grey #8C8C8C lines. No shapes with corner radii, no shadows, no gradients other than the photo overlay.

### Slide 28

**Text**

WHERE TERRESTRIAL INFRASTRUCTURE / FALLS SHORT / LOW POPULATION DENSITY / REMOTE COMMUNITIES / CHALLENGING TOPOGRAPHY / DEEP-SEA ENVIRONMENTS / AIRBORNE ALTITUDE / DISASTER ZONES / 28

**Layout**

Six photographs sit in a 3-by-2 grid of equal boxes with a small uppercase tracked label above each box, below a two-line left-aligned title at the top left. The grid is left-aligned with the title at about 5% of slide width and spans roughly 9% to 91% of width, with the photo rows at about 31% and 64% of slide height. A small logo sits bottom left and the page number bottom right, both on a plain black field.

**Visuals**

Six photographs, none with a chart or table. (1) Top left: a red tractor on bare reddish-brown earth beside a large tree with yellow blossoms, a child in a yellow-green hi-vis jacket standing with both arms raised, blue sky, flat farmland behind; daylight, wide framing, boxed with square corners. (2) Top centre: snow-covered flat ground with blowing snow, a dark blue-grey dome tent and a grey cylindrical structure, several thin flag poles with red and green flags, and a grey solar panel on a stand in the foreground; overcast, cold white-grey light, boxed. (3) Top right: jagged snow-capped mountains in teal-grey behind a brown semi-arid plain with scrub, rock outcrops and a dirt road with a small dark vehicle; hazy sky, boxed. (4) Bottom left: a white-grey hover craft with black railings and yellow-black hazard stripes moving fast across dark water with white spray at its bow, angled three-quarter view from above; boxed. (5) Bottom centre: close side view of an aircraft fuselage with oval windows, a wing and a dark engine nacelle printed SPACEX, the tail fin in gold-brown and black hexagonal pattern with a white logo; grey sky, crop of the aircraft, boxed. (6) Bottom right: aerial view of a flooded river valley with muddy debris, white industrial buildings, a red roof in the foreground, a brown river channel and green forested hills; overcast daylight, boxed. Logo: white SpaceX-style X mark bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000. Pure black background with no gradient or texture. Title in white uppercase sans-serif (GS Sans-like) regular weight, widely spaced, about 7.6% of slide height per em, two lines with tight leading. Photo labels are white uppercase bold sans-serif with wide letter-spacing, about 2% of slide height. No accent colour. Photos have square corners and no borders or shadows. Page number is white small sans-serif. Margins are about 5% left and right.

29

**Layout**

Three-column layout on a black slide: a large all-caps title spans two lines across the top (left-aligned at about 5% margin), followed by three vertical panels separated by thin grey vertical rules at about x 38.9% and x 70.7%, running from about y 25% to y 92%. Left panel is a bar chart (x 5 to 37%), middle panel is a column chart (x 41 to 69%), right panel is a stacked list of stat rows with horizontal rules (x 72 to 98%). Footer: Starlink logo bottom-left, a two-line source note bottom-centre-left starting at x 9.5%, and the page number 29 at bottom-right (x 97%); all footer text is small and light grey.

**Visuals**

Left: vertical bar chart titled UNRIVALED SATELLITE INFRASTRUCTURE, subtitle SATELLITES IN ORBIT AS OF MARCH 31, 2026. Y-axis labelled with tick values 2,000 to 12,000 in steps of 2,000 (no unit printed; satellites). Bars: SpaceX bar white (#FFFFFF), value 9,600+ printed above, bar reaches about 9,600; OTHERS bar light grey (#D9D9D9), value ~3,200 printed above, reaching about 3,200. Bottom labels: SpaceX logo (white wordmark) and OTHERS. Callouts beside bars: ~75% with caption Of Active Maneuverable Satellites in Orbit; 3,000+ with caption Deployed in 2025. No legend; axis has no visible vertical line, horizontal gridlines at each tick in light grey. Middle: column chart titled EXPANDING USER BASE, subtitle STARLINK SUBSCRIBERS (M). Y-axis 2 to 12 in steps of 2 (millions of subscribers, labelled M). Columns white (#FFFFFF): 2023 = 2.3 M, 2024 = 4.4 M, 2025 = 8.9 M, Q1 2026 = 10.3 M, values printed above each column. Inside the base of columns 2024, 2025 and Q1 2026 are growth labels in near-black text: 97%, 100%, 105% labelled with the legend-like box % YoY Growth at bottom-left of the chart (dark translucent box); no growth value shown for 2023. Horizontal light grey gridlines at each tick. Right: stat panel with five rows of label and large white figure, each row separated by thin grey horizontal rules: Countries With Coverage 164; People Covered 3.3B+; Average Uptime 99.9%; Median Download Speed (Mbps) 225 with small italic note Average Terrestrial ISPs: 120 Mbps; Median Round Trip Latency (Millisecond) ~25 with small italic note Average Terrestrial ISPs: 7-34 milliseconds. Logo: Starlink mark (white stylised swoosh) at bottom-left corner.

**Design**

Background: Black #000000 with a faint dark night-time satellite photograph of the Earth (city lights on continents) behind all content. Dark, cinematic look: black base (#000000) with a faint night-time satellite-view map of the Earth in the background. Titles, figures and labels are white #FFFFFF; accent bars are white #FFFFFF and secondary bar is light grey #D9D9D9; footer text and OTHERS label are mid grey #A6A6A6 to #BFBFBF; rules are grey #7F7F7F at about 0.1% of slide height. Typeface: geometric sans-serif similar to GS Sans / Gotham family; title is regular weight all caps, roughly 7.5% of slide height per em (cap height about 5.3% of height); section headers bold all caps about 2.7%; subtitles regular all caps about 2.1%; body labels regular about 2.0% to 2.6%; large stat numerals about 5.5% to 6% of height; footer about 1.1%. Shapes are plain rectangles with square corners, no shadows, no gradients. Chart values use light weight numerals.

**Layout**

Full-width title block in the top-left spanning two lines, with five equal-width vertical columns below it (Fixed Site, Land Mobility, Maritime, Aviation, Starshield), each with a bold heading above a tall photo and a dark slate logo panel beneath. A centred two-line statement sits below the columns, with a small logo at bottom-left and the page number at bottom-right. Left margin about 4.2% and right margin about 4%; columns are about 16.9% wide with gaps of about 2%.

**Visuals**

Five photographs in tall boxed columns, each with a dark slate logo panel below. (1) Fixed Site: a dusk sky in pale pink-blue fading to dusky mauve at the top; a rust-brown corrugated wall runs diagonally from lower-left to right, with a small solar panel on a thin pole at upper-right of the wall; cropped, boxed, no overlay; dominant colours mauve, rust, slate. Logos on panel: KDDI and Casey's in white. (2) Land Mobility: snow-capped mountains in pale blue-white under a cloudy sky, brown desert rocks in the foreground, and a metallic grey-silver Tesla Cybertruck driving on a dirt road, seen from the side and slightly low; boxed; dominant colours pale blue, tan, grey. Logos on panel: John Deere, .italo, brightline, CAL FIRE shield, all white. (3) Maritime: a white-and-grey hovercraft with a black skirt moving fast across grey water, with motion blur on the wake and water, spray lit white at the bow; boxed; dominant colours grey, white, slate. Logos on panel: Royal Caribbean, Maersk, Carnival, Norwegian Cruise Line, MSC Cruises, all white. (4) Aviation: a grey-and-white single-aisle airliner with a black tail and white registration, banking in a blue sky with white clouds; boxed; dominant colours sky blue, white, grey. Logos on panel: Emirates, AIRFRANCE, Southwest, QATAR AIRWAYS, Alaska, LUFTHANSA GROUP, UNITED, BRITISH AIRWAYS, all white. (5) Starshield: a full-bleed photo of Earth's limb with bright white snow and blue atmosphere at top, and a grey-and-black spacecraft or robotic structure with cables and panels filling the centre; a dark gradient overlay darkens the lower part where white text sits. Logos on panel: Gobierno de México seal, FEMA seal, National Park Service arrowhead, Ecuador flag in grey and white. Every logo is white or light grey.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 background.

**Layout**

Full-bleed dark slide with a title block across the top spanning about 80 percent of the width and a left margin of about 4.4 percent. Two stat columns labelled GEN 1 and GEN 2 sit in the left half, each stat row with a number at left, a right-aligned label, and a thin rule beneath; the right half holds a photograph with a two-paragraph text block in its lower right. Footnote and small logo sit bottom left and the slide number bottom right.

**Visuals**

Photograph on the right side, spanning x 35 to 100 percent and full height, with a left-edge gradient fading from black into the photo. Subject: a man with glasses and a dark green backpack, wearing a white shirt and dark trousers, sits in a varnished wooden canoe on a still turquoise lake, holding a smartphone up to photograph the scene; green forest foliage fills the top edge and is reflected in the water. Composition: the canoe runs diagonally from lower left to upper right, subject at about upper-middle of the frame, full-bleed, uncropped at the right and top edges. Lighting: soft natural daylight. Dominant colours: turquoise #1FA59A, forest green #3E7A3A, amber wood #B8803A, black #000000. A dark gradient overlay covers the lower band of the slide. Thin grey horizontal rules, about 0.1 percent height, in #8A8A8A, sit under GEN 1 ~30, GEN 1 ~1.9B, GEN 2 5G and GEN 2 65MHZ. No chart, table or other graphic. Small white X-style logo bottom left.

### Slide 32

**Text**

OUR AI / BUSINESS
We are the only company that can vertically integrate across / AI compute infrastructure, frontier model, and real-time data
We are building our AI business by leveraging our / infrastructure DNA, foundational launch capabilities, and / global connectivity network

**Layout**

Single left-aligned text column on the left third of the slide, with a large title block at about 41% from the top and a two-paragraph body beneath it, left edge about 3.8% from the slide edge. No footer, no slide number, no header. The full-bleed photograph fills the whole slide behind the text, and a small white logo sits at the bottom-left corner.

**Visuals**

One full-bleed photograph of Earth's limb at sunrise seen from orbit. A bright sun flare sits above the horizon at about x 52%, y 37%, with a faint lens ghost to its right. The horizon curves from the left edge (about y 22%) down to the right edge (about y 62%). Two long dark solar array panels extend across the frame: the left panel runs from about x 8%, y 30% down to x 46%, y 52%, and the right panel runs from about x 49%, y 49% down to x 92%, y 73%, both with visible cell grids. A small folded satellite body with a white-grey frame sits between the panels at about x 43%, y 48%, 5% wide and 18% tall. Composition is wide landscape framing with the planet's atmosphere glowing blue along the limb and grey-white cloud texture below. Lighting is a strong backlight from the sun. Dominant colours are black, deep navy blue, white and warm amber glare. The photograph is not cropped and not boxed; a dark gradient darkens the left side behind the text for legibility. The file also contains a 450 by 540 pixel image and several 2 by 2 pixel images placed in the left area, which are not visibly distinct on the rendered slide.

**Design**

Background: Full-bleed photograph of Earth and a solar-array satellite on a black (#000000) field. Black background. Title in white (#FFFFFF), uppercase, light-weight geometric grotesque sans-serif (close to GS Sans Light), with slightly open letter-spacing, two lines, cap height about 5.3% of slide height. Body in off-white (#F2F2F2) regular-weight grotesque sans-serif, about 2.5% of slide height, with generous line spacing and a blank line between paragraphs. No shapes, lines, borders, corner radii or shadows. Accent is limited to the white logo.

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph with a two-line headline block across the top, left-aligned at about 4.9% from the left edge. Below the photograph, four equal-width stat columns sit side by side, separated by three thin vertical hairlines, each stat centred on its column with a large figure above a two- or three-line caption. The footnote runs along the bottom left beside a small logo mark, and the page number sits at the bottom right at about 97% from the left edge.

**Visuals**

One full-bleed aerial photograph of a large energy-storage and compute site, shown in the background of the whole slide. Subject: a flat, pale tan gravel yard with hundreds of small grey and white cylindrical battery units in rows, a long low white-grey industrial building with a canopy on its right side, a dark blue solar panel array in the upper left of the yard, green fields and a dirt road on the left, a dark tree line and a grey river or lake across the middle distance, and electrical substation equipment and cable racks at the right edge. Composition is a high oblique aerial view with the site filling the lower two thirds and sky occupying the top third. Lighting is soft, low-contrast daylight, dimmed by an overlay. Dominant colours are slate blue-grey #2B3A46, tan #B9A98A and muted green #3D5A3A. The photo is not cropped and is not boxed. A dark gradient overlay (near-black #0B1116 at the top, fading out by about 40% of the slide height, and darker at the bottom) tints the whole image. Three vertical hairline dividers, light grey #C8CDD3 at about 1 px, separate the stat columns. A small white Grok-style X logo mark sits at bottom left.

**Design**

Background: Dark aerial photograph of the site under a near-black #0B1116 to transparent gradient, over a #000000 base fill. Cinematic, high-contrast look. Background is a dark photograph under a dark gradient overlay. Headline and subtitle are white #FFFFFF in a regular-weight geometric grotesk sans-serif (GS Sans style), set in uppercase with slight positive letter-spacing. The title is about 7.8% of slide height and the subtitle about 4.8%. Stat figures are white #FFFFFF in the same sans at about 5.6% of slide height, with the 'st' superscript smaller. Captions are white #FFFFFF, regular sans, about 2.3% of slide height, sentence case, centred. Footnote and page number are white at about 1.3% and 1.5% of slide height. Accents are thin light grey #C8CDD3 hairlines. No corner radii, shadows or rounded shapes.
